# God of the Ant Farm — Stabilization milestone: audit notes & bug ledger

## 1. Architecture map (discovered)

80 js files, ~51k lines, one global `G`, all state in `G.S` (one world). Scripts load in index.html order (util → … → main → devmode).

### Simulation step (main.js `step(dt)`, dt ≤ 0.1 s; DAY_LEN = 200 s = 1 year)
1. S.clock, S.time (slows ×0.35 while a scene is watched); day rollover → `Village.onNewDay` (expansion, politics daily, …)
2. `Nature.update` (trees, fire, weather, clouds)
3. `Village.update` → faith, **`Fac.update`** → [Politics, WorldWar, War (bands), Siege, Civ, City, **Naval** (→ Know, Fishers, Expe, Fleets), Sea, **Stories** (+Party tick, Riches poll), Eco, Army, Wars, Fest]; farms/ruins/milestones (1 s), housing (2.5 s), jobs (4 s), plan (3.5 s; empty settlement → abandon)
4. `Vg.updateAll` (villager AI, tasks, movement, emergencies, babies, watchdog)
5. Life, Carnage, Animals.updateAll, Flora, Riches, Estates, Court, Trade, Migrate, Nests, **Polity, Justice, Riots, Secrets, Scene**, Caves, Pets, Powers, Lore, Events
Frame (rAF): Cinema, Photo, Scene.frame, Track.frame, Render, FX, Sky; Skip runs slices via MessageChannel instead.

### Persistence
- `Save.serialize`: core arrays (RLE/zero-run/delta), trees/rocks/bushes, buildings, villagers (**task, path, emo deleted; coup/rebel/revolt zeroed; inside → door**), dead, animals, settlements, factions (rel deduped by id, coup/rev/revolt/exec nulled), history, stats, weather…
- `saveHooks` (save/load per module): animals(eco), biomes, carnage, caves, city, economy(ores), expeditions(exps, castaways), fleets, flora, justice(execs, scaffolds), lore, migrate, miracles, naval, nests, pets, riots, sea, seaknow, secrets, siege(walls), stories(saga), trade(luxRoutes), wars(wz → resume: armies re-muster), wild, worldwar.
- NOT persisted: War.bands (raids/defence bands), Scene.list (running scenes), Fest live map (festivals in progress), task state of every villager.

### Cross-module glue
- Story engine observes the world by **wrapping** functions in `St.init` (Village.kill, War.capture/freeCaptive/startEscape/foundFree/conquer/onPeace, Politics.extinct/declareWar/crown, Animals.kill) and by explicit `St.signal(kind, d)` calls (~40 kinds).
- Dev mode wraps St.signal, console.warn/error, and (on demand) module update functions.

| D-009 | design | Social | the rich lived wherever the house assignment put them, so a "noble quarter" could form on cheap land | done (UR): once a day in each city, a rich family on cheap ground buys the house of a plain family on dear ground (coin, pantry, goods and a merchant house move with each family); urb1 #12 |

| T-013 | test | urb2 #3 | a garden forced onto one adult who builds late, sails and feasts was never tended in the window | the test now watches the town's own gardens, as they come |

| T-018 | test | Sea t17 #8 | "an island known only to whoever learned of it" needs the expedition to find a new island and bring the news home: in the urban round's world (seed 7) the Great Expedition came back having found nothing new, and on seed 11 the old build (before the urban round) fails it the same way; seed 3 passes | world-specific; the mechanism is unchanged (14, 9 and 15 still pass) |

| T-017 | test | urb1 #12, urb2 #2 | #12 read the quarters' snapshot right after a load (a load clears it until the next step); #2 measured the drinkers' house coin, which the day's wages raise | #12 steps a few ticks first; #2 counts what the tavern took in |
| L-072 | S2 | Memory/Save | the dead were never forgotten and cemeteries never reused: every death stayed in the registry forever (`S.dead`) and every twelve deaths opened a new cemetery — a world of a thousand years would keep tens of thousands of strangers (heavier than the world itself, too big for the browser's save) and hundreds of cemeteries; found while planning the 1000-year ready-made world | fixed (TS round): `Village.forget` at each turn of the year (strangers after 80 years; rulers, heroes, those with an epithet, great killers and the parents, grandparents and late spouse of the living stay); `Village.cemeteryFor` reuses the oldest cemetery once its last burial is 30 years old or the town has as many as its size calls for (2 + pop/60), the bones to the ossuary; `Village.bury` never lays a thirteenth stone (the next cemetery, or the common pit); checker `mem` (judged at the moment the world forgets), scenario `qa/memoria.js` |
| T-021 | test | checker | the checker called an army walking home after the peace "active in a war that ended" (S1): walking home is how a peace is carried out (`Wars.onPeace` sends the band home; the army is closed when the band arrives, on the screen too) — seen only when the checker looks every year (the long lives look every five); first blamed on the skip's new step | the checker now accepts an army going home after its war (one still marching, camped, besieging or fighting stays S1) and warns (S3) if the way home takes more than two years |
| T-022 | test | checker | the save round trip settled homes and jobs once before comparing, but the jobs move at most three people a town per round: a growing metropolis (526 people) still had moves left, and five changed trade after the load — seen on the ready-made metropolis | the round trip settles until nothing moves (up to 30 rounds); the metropolis then comes back identical, twice |
| D-010 | design | Time skip | the skip lives in two rhythms: people, fights, armies, ships and fire in half-steps of 0.5 s (as before), beasts, plants, the planner, politics and the economy in steps of 1 s; homes and trades dealt half as often; beasts nobody is near (no person within 8–16 tiles, outside every town) live every other step with the time added up, never while hunting, fleeing or eating; an animal step longer than 0.9 tile is walked in pieces (a coarse step could otherwise hop a narrow river — the screen never gets there); the slow step runs between the two quick halves, so what it decides (a peace, a town given up) is acted on in the same step, as on the screen; during the skip a sleeper in bed lives every other half-step (the time added up), and the head counts (`Fac.pop`, `Village.pop`, `Fac.has`, `Village.cap`, `hasWell`) are taken once per part of a step (`G.perPart`) | ~30% less time per skipped year on the same world under the same load; same kind of world in 3 seeds × 30 years (people, cities, wars, the wild born and dead); animals out of place as rare as on the screen |
| D-011 | design | Ready-made worlds | the "instant" jump to a later age is a save shipped with the game, lived by the game's own skip (`qa/prontos.js`), not a formula that guesses the years: the same rules, the same future. One lineage gives 100, 500 and 1000 years (and the moment its world war began, if one does); the first city, metropolis and megalopolis come from the blessed skip, each on its own map | `qa/mundos.js` opens each one through the menu, compares it with the list, lives it, saves and loads it, checks the autosave fits |
| D-012 | design | Performance | a world of 1370 people had 1108 different object layouts (each person's fields came in its own order over a life): every read in the hot loops went the slow way. Every person now has one layout (`Vg.shape`, at birth and on load: the same keys in the same order, those never set held empty); the animals' template holds the fields that come later in a life | the 1400-person world: a skipped year 33 → 22 s, a step on the screen 40 → 31 ms; the same world to the last bit with and without it (the checker's canon, every saved person, the world's chance); guard rule 6 (never ask whether a person "has" a key) |
| L-074 | S2 | Performance | a world left to itself outgrows what the game can run: the 160-map lineage reached 1300 people at 140 years, a step on the screen took 40 ms (the world ran at a third of real time here) and a skipped year 30 s — found generating the 1000-year world | partly fixed (TS round): D-010, D-012 (a step 31 ms, a skipped year 21 s); the 1000-year ready world lives on a 128 map. Still open: the cost of each person (needs, tasks, paths) |
| L-075 | S2 | Sea | a merchant ship sailed over the corner of a rocky shore: the sea path is smoothed into straight lines, and the line was checked at points every 0.3 of a tile, so a line that only grazed a land corner passed (the ship stood 0.09 inside the rock) — on the screen too (6 times in 200 looks at a 100-year world; 2 in the skip); found by the checker on the 100-year ready world | fixed (TS round): the line walks the grid and checks every tile it crosses (both tiles beside a corner it passes through); the same 200 looks: 0 and 0 |
| L-076 | S2 | Save | a big world could not be saved: the browser keeps about 5.2 million characters for the site, and a world of 1600 souls is more as plain text — the autosave failed every 45 s and "Continue" brought back an older world; the menu also parsed the whole world just to show its day | fixed (TS round): a world over 3 million characters is saved gzip-compressed by the browser itself (`CompressionStream`), a moment later (`Save.pending`); a short note beside it tells the menu the day and the people; "Continue" opens it (`Save.read`); the 1363-person world: 4.4 MB → 1.0 MB, 0.25 s of pause; `corrupt.js` cases: a good compressed save loads, a cut one, garbage and compressed garbage are refused cleanly (the garbage one first threw — fixed); `mundos.js` comes back through "Continue" |
| L-077 | S2 | City | no city ever became a megalopolis: a block (quarteirão, needed three times) was allowed only where the land is worth 62, and in a metropolis that land is where its palace, temple and square stand — measured on the ready metropolis (Tenochtitlan, 211 souls, 20 blessed years on): not one 4×4 piece of its heart was free or held only houses, the rest of its reach ran into five neighbouring towns, and its homes (1×1, each with a free ring) only ever grew in place; the planner, besides, asks for a home only when beds run short, and a full town grows upwards instead — so every block it asked for came down to a lower house, and the skip's goal "until the first megalopolis" could never be met (the ready megalopolis stopped at year 142, rank 4, 1800 souls) | fixed (TS round): a block stands where an ínsula may (land worth 52: it is ínsulas round a yard); it may pull down, in its 2×2 and the ring around it, up to four low houses, sobrados, yard gardens and fields of its own town — never anything civic, never an ínsula — and must bring more beds than it takes (24 of its 40); a metropolis with ínsulas and stone to spare rebuilds its heart in blocks as one of the ways it grows upwards (as many as 60% of its people fill), one at a time; the heart of a big city is dearer (rank 3 adds 5 at its centre, a metropolis 10, fading to nothing at the edge). A new invariant: no blocking building touches a block (its ring stays walkable). Scenario `qa/quarteirao.js` |

| L-126 | S2 | Alcance / náufragos | quem fica preso num pedaço da própria terra de onde não chega a pé à cidade não tinha saída. Na regressão (o deus caótico, caos 7), o deus afundou a terra em volta do cais de Tenochtitlan e a pescadora Izel ficou no bolsão do cais (os barcos a deixavam onde embarcava). Três defeitos: (1) a regra dos desgarrados pulava o governante — Izel virou governante no mesmo dia e ficou "morando onde não chega a pé" por dias (S2); (2) os náufragos decidiam a volta pela "mesma terra" do mapa do mar (`K.at`), não pelo mapa de aonde se anda — no bolsão ao lado de casa "voltavam do naufrágio" todo dia sem dar um passo; (3) achado no caminho, com o conserto de (2) pela metade (caos 13): os náufragos de um povo que acabou enquanto estavam fora fundavam uma aldeia para o povo extinto (S1). Existia desde a regra dos desgarrados (964eb8a); a vida da v33 não passava por ali | quem está num bolsão perto de casa é posto onde alcança a cidade a pé, como os travados (`Vg.unstick`), na regra dos desgarrados e na dos náufragos; o náufrago volta só para uma cidade dos seus aonde chega a pé (`W.reachesTown`); o governante isolado não vira hóspede nem cativo — vai para uma cidade do seu povo nesta terra, ou espera como náufrago; o náufrago de um povo extinto é do povo que acolheu os últimos dele, e nenhuma aldeia nasce para um povo que não existe (`strays` no `village.js`, `castaways` no `expeditions.js`). Prova nova `qa/ilhado.js` (governante, morador e náufrago em bolsões de penhasco ao lado da cidade; o governante numa terra de estranhos): falha no código antigo ("o náufrago voltou para casa sem sair do bolsão, 7 vezes"), passa agora; caos 7 e caos 13 limpos |

Na regressão (encarna e8, a metrópole depois do salto de 10 anos): no dia 63 a Pólis de Argos tomou Kaupang e fez cativos 15 do Clã de Hrafn; no mesmo dia Kaupang se levantou contra os ocupantes e voltou para o Clã de Hrafn — e os 15 ficaram cativos dentro da própria cidade, do próprio povo (Erik, Gunnar, Hilda…; a sonda que vigia cada módulo de cada passo achou o momento: o `Wars`, o levante). Quatro caminhos levavam a isso e nenhum soltava: (1) a cidade tomada que se rebela e volta para o seu povo (`Politics.revert`); (2) a ocupada que se levanta contra os ocupantes (a ocupação das guerras); (3) a ocupada devolvida na paz (`Wars.onPeace`) — a troca de prisioneiros logo depois procura cativos de um povo numa cidade do outro, e eles já estavam em casa; (4) o cativo que naufraga e chega a uma cidade do seu povo virava "hóspede" (e continuava cativo), ou era feito cativo de novo pelos seus, como se fosse dos captores. Só a conquista soltava os cativos do vencedor. Existia desde a ocupação (WE) e os náufragos (MD); a e8 da v33 não passava por ali | uma porta: `G.War.homecoming(set)` solta os cativos do povo da cidade que moram nela (a vida conta "voltou a ser livre"; a crônica, quantos), chamada logo ali pelos três caminhos que devolvem uma cidade; o cativo náufrago que chega a uma cidade do seu povo está em casa, e livre (`strangerAt` no `expeditions.js`). Regra nova 11 no `guard.js`: quem muda o povo de uma cidade chama `homecoming` logo ali, ou está na lista com o motivo (a conquista solta no próprio laço; o racha cria um povo que não tem cativos; navios, histórias e a janela não são cidades) — acusa os três caminhos no código antigo. Prova nova `qa/cativos.js` (no mundo pronto «guerra mundial»: a revolta, o levante e a paz branca, com um cativo de um terceiro povo que continua cativo, e salvar e carregar; num arquipélago novo, o náufrago): 6 falhas no código antigo, tudo passa agora. A e8 com a sonda deu os mesmos nomes da regressão (o mundo é determinístico) |
| L-150 | S2 | Encarnado (o corpo e a água) | um prédio que nasce sobre um vau prendia o corpo dentro dele para sempre: a regressão da v36 falhou na e9 "um prédio nasce em cima" ({"onIt":true,"walk":false}) só nas rodadas inteiras, e passava sozinha. Reprodução: a sonda na sequência inteira (e1 a e7, e9) mostrou a pessoa escolhida de pé num vau (rio raso, que se anda) e a marca de quem nada (`task.afloat: 1`) logo depois de a casa nascer; nas rodadas curtas a pessoa escolhida estava em terra. Causa: o corpo se marcava nadando onde "não se anda e há água embaixo" (`avatar.js`: `!W.walkableXY && waterAt > -∞`) — mas o que não deixava andar ali era o prédio, não a água; e o mundo não tira de onde está quem nada pela vontade do jogador (D-026), então o corpo ficava dentro das paredes. O `Space.surface` dizia 'deep' (água funda) num prédio sobre a água pelo mesmo motivo: olhava a água antes do prédio. Correção: num tile tomado por um prédio o corpo não é nadador (`!W.blocked`), e o `Space.surface` diz 'building' antes de 'deep' (o 3D colide com o prédio, o Encarnar não vê água funda ali). Nada da simulação das pessoas lê essas duas coisas. Prova: a parte nova da e9, "um prédio nasce em cima de um vau" (o corpo posto num vau, a casa ali): no código antigo FALHOU (`afloat: true`, dentro da casa), no novo PASSOU; e a fase inteira do Modo Encarnado de novo |

## Details

### L-001 Sonho never born
- Observed: 0 dream seeds in 60 years (seed 7 and seed 11), director loose.
- Measured (seed 7, 25 y): 8 tales by the fire, 43 children, 19 with the right temperament; the 'conto' fact reached the archetype **0 times**.
- Root cause: OBS.tale → placeOfTale(tale) returns a place only for chronicle events with a sea/cave/wonder/meteor/mountain icon or within 8 tiles of a named relief place. Most tales are the elder's own memories (bio, weight 4 each), which carry no place even when they are about the sea (voyage, discovered, wrecked, castaway, colonist, sawLand). For 'mar' the dream target is recomputed from the child's home (coastFor), so a sea memory is enough.
- Fix: sea memories carry ic 'sea'; placeOfTale's sea branch falls back to the nearest sea anywhere.

### L-002 NaN in stock
- Observed: Pólis de Argos/Clã de Hrafn stock.water = NaN at year 5 (soak seed 7 continente 96).
- Root cause: V.addStock(k,n): add = max(0, min(n, cap - f.stock[k])) with f.stock[k] undefined → NaN. Water carried from the well (v.carry {k:'water'}) is dumped into the store by every "drop what you carry" path (band return, army supply, trade, war call-up).
- Fix: addStock ignores water (never a store good) and non-positive/NaN amounts and treats a missing key as 0; load sanitizes non-finite stock values (old saves).

### L-003 Herds turn wild on load
- Observed: pastors (job curral) become guerreiro/oleiro after a save→load; canon diff on role/job.
- Root cause: the animal serialization in save.js omits dom, pen, wool, milk, eggs, shorn, tended; spawn defaults dom=0, pen=0 → every livestock animal becomes wild; pens are empty; jobs are reassigned.
- Fix: serialize the herd fields; restore state 'pen'.

### L-004 Ids burned on load
- Root cause: Sv.apply → Animals.spawn(kind,x,y,saved) runs id: S.nextId++ before the saved id overrides it.
- Fix: restore S.nextId after the animals are recreated.


## 3. Candidate risks to test
- R5 (unconfirmed): one non-deterministic regression run (w10 medium) recorded a battle where one side lost 117 of 118 with none wounded, captured or fled, during a guerrilla phase. Mechanism suspected: out of a formal battle a company regains morale fast (+0.3/s), so a guerrilla rarely breaks. Not reproduced: deterministic w10 medium ×4 (both builds) and 4 forced guerrillas (one broke and fled at 60% losses, the rest ended on the 70 s clock). A fix is prepared (qa/fix6_guerrilla.py: melt away after losing 2 in 5) but NOT applied without a repro.
- R1 save/load: armies re-muster on load (phase → mobilizar); siege continuity?
- R2 save/load: raid/defence bands (War.bands) are not saved → soldiers mid-raid lose their band; captives being escorted lose 'escorted'.
- R3 save/load: running scenes and festivals in progress are dropped.
- R4 villager tasks dropped on save: aboard/condemned/jail/escorted/scene restored by whom?

## 4. Harness notes
- Deterministic runs: init script seeds Math.random, freezes Date.now, stubs requestAnimationFrame before page scripts (the menu world never runs), reseeds at each chunk (music timers draw random numbers between chunks). Verified: 1500 steps identical; a 6-year soak with save/load cycles identical across two runs.
- saveloop.js: serialize→apply ×3: the save is a fixed point except per-villager AI jitter (think/scan, re-randomized on load by design) and default fields (hood, dz, robe, sac, wind, stormT).
- genmx.js: 40 worlds per run over 5 map types × 6 sizes × 5 climates × 5 reliefs × tempers × 1–6 peoples: no exceptions, no NaN heights, no animals in water; 1 world in 40 had 1–2 starting people in shallow sea (to investigate).
- T-005: sea test 8 ("ilha conhecida só por quem soube") needs the expedition to land somewhere new; with t17 made deterministic (DET=1: frozen clock, no menu frames), the pre-batch-4 build also fails it on seed 7 (the expedition comes back from an empty sea). A flaky test by design, not a regression — judge it over several seeds (DET seeds 3/7/11: old build 2/3, new 1/3; the phase traces of both builds show the same failing path: voyage → course ends with no region → a new leg cannot be plotted → home, "Não trouxe nada além de histórias do mar").
- T-006: "bando N: membro morto/inexistente" (S1) can be seen once right after a death: a raid band prunes its list on the war tick (every 0.5 s), so a death between two ticks is listed for < 0.5 s. A checker artifact, not a lasting state (chaos run: 1 hit in 213 iterations).
- Checker: castaways (v.wrecked) are not "living where they cannot walk" — the expeditions module re-tasks them (camp / native town / home); they were a false positive after a save (tasks are not saved).
- performance.memory is bucketed and cached by Chromium unless `--enable-precise-memory-info` is passed: heap figures from runs before that flag (ev.js/soak.js now carry it) are coarse (the 15-world loop read 23.1 MB every time). Re-measured with the flag in the memory phase.
- QA.canon drops `ug` (underground): a save never keeps anyone in a cave or mine — load brings them out at the mouth by design (caves.js load hook), so it showed up as a save/load "diff" in every long soak.

## 5. Foundations (reforms after the stabilization)
Three sources of whole families of bugs were rebuilt so that new systems cannot bring them back. Each one ships with its own checker invariant, a scenario test, and (where a rule can be read off the code) a static guard.

| ID | Reform | What changed | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|
| RF-1 | Where one can walk to | `W.reachIds()`: the walkable tiles labelled by 4-connected flood (findPath walks exactly this graph); a shut gate counts as a way through. Every change of the ground announces itself (`W.touchWalk()` on buildings up/down, ruins, walls, lava on a bridge; `S.typeVer` on terrain), with a 20 s safety rebuild. `W.canWalk` answers what findPath would, and findPath fails at once on another region (impossible searches 118 ms → 0.2 ms per 30). Strays, captives, hunting, gathering, migrations, caves, mulberries, nests and the stories ask `W.reaches / reachesTown / townReaches / townsLinked` instead of "same island". | checker S1 "mapa de alcance desatualizado" (a fresh flood against the map in use); "não chega a pé" uses the town's region; `reach.js` (500/500 pairs agree with the search; wall ring, gates, a building in the gap, an unannounced change caught) |
| RF-2 | Who is aboard | `v.aboard` is the one truth; `s.crew` is only the ship's roster. Everyone gets on and off through `Naval.board(v, s)` / `Naval.unboard(v, p, keepTask)` (30 places used to write it by hand); `Naval.aboardOf(s)` reads an index derived from `v.aboard` (rebuilt when someone boards or steps off, with a 5 s safety rebuild), in roster order. Boarding another ship takes the person off the old roster. | checker S1 "índice de quem está a bordo desatualizado"; `aboard.js` (index = plain count on every ship; board, change ships, step off, a sinking; an unannounced boarding caught); `guard.js` rule 1 (no direct writes of `.aboard`). Exact comparison with RF-3 on: RF-1+3 and RF-1+2+3 live the same 3 years (archipelago, 5 peoples) |
| RF-3 | One chance per system | The world draws from its own generator (`G.R/rr/ri/pick/chance`), seeded by the world's seed, kept in `S.rng` and in the save (a load resumes it exactly; what loading itself draws comes from a generator of its own). What only shows or sounds draws from `G.VR/vrr/vri/vpick/vchance` (render, FX, audio, cinema, sky, the drawing of buildings, a scene's smoke, the cave critters). No `Math.random` anywhere but the seeds. 53 module clocks and 9 module lists/marks start over in `G.worldHooks` (L-021); bands and companies take ids from `S.nextId`. Drawing no longer changes the world: a beast drawn asleep in its den, a person held by the hand, keep their state; the economy's building index and the fence map are rebuilt only by the world (a question from the camera between steps is answered afresh); the sun lingers while a scene is happening, watched or not (it used to linger only while watched, so watching changed the hour). The dev mode shows the world's seed, makes it again, and takes a seed for the next one (D-004). | `det3.js` (no frames × frames + camera + cinema + sound + panels; the same seed twice in one page; a save loaded twice), `detloc.js` (the step and the line where two runs part), `rngtrace.js`; checker S1 "o acaso do mundo se perdeu"; `guard.js` rule 3 (no `Math.random`; drawing, sound and UI files never draw the world's chance). The old DET harness was not bit-exact (T-010 and the 'dead 11' run that passed and failed on the same build); now every run of a seed is the same run |
